### Assistant

_source line 1261 · 2026-05-21T00:37:13.832Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is critical information. The README says:
- `base_model: Qwen/Qwen3-8B-Base` — meaning Qwen3-8B is NOT the base model, it's built on top of Qwen3-8B-Base
- "Training Stage: Pretraining & Post-training" — it went through post-training (instruction tuning/RLHF)
- It supports "thinking mode" and "non-thinking mode" for instruction following
- "Superior human preference alignment" and "instruction following"

So **Qwen3-8B IS already an instruct model**, despite not having "Instruct" in the name. 

The naming scheme here breaks from Qwen2.5's pattern — the base model is "Qwen3-8B-Base" while the instruction-tuned version is simply "Qwen3-8B". This means the worker was right that "Qwen3-8B-Instruct" doesn't exist; the instruct version is just "Qwen3-8B" without any suffix. The experiment_plan.yaml was pointing to a non-existent model ID, when it should've been using "Qwen/Qwen3-8B" instead.

So all four models end up being instruction-tuned versions, which eliminates any base versus instruct confound in the experiment. I need to update the experiment_plan.yaml with the correct model name and fix the local path reference in generate_design.py for Qwen.
```

</details>

But the shard expected values: each shard shows 64,800 expected. 

There's a discrepancy here — if each shard has 299 conditions (300 lines minus header), that's 59,800 records per shard, totaling 239,200 across four shards. Yet the worker reports 64,800 per shard, suggesting 324 conditions each, which would mean the pilot conditions are being duplicated across the shards. The discrepancy points to the shards being regenerated with all 1,296 conditions rather than the 299 I counted earlier — the math checks out: 259 completed conditions divided by 324 total equals the 79.9% progress the worker is reporting. Either I miscounted the original shard file or the shards have been regenerated since I checked them.

Looking at the worker's numbers more carefully, it's reporting 208,200 total records out of 259,200 expected, which matches the full Llama design size. The worker might be aggregating pilot data from a separate directory along with the shard outputs, so I need to verify whether those four shard totals actually sum to the reported progress. hard file sizes only. If 208,200 / 259,200 = 80.3%, the worker assumes the shard files should contain all 259,200 records. But the shards only cover 1,196 conditions (239,200 records) + pilot covers 100 conditions (20,000 records) separately.

So either the shards DO contain 1,296 conditions (re-running pilot conditions), or the worker's expected total is wrong. The actual numbers suggest 208,200 records across 4 shards, with per-shard expected of 64,800 = 324 × 200. This means 324 conditions per shard = 1,296 total conditions in shards, which would mean the pilot's 100 conditions are being re-run.

This isn't necessarily a problem — having duplicate data for the pilot's float32+T=0.0 conditions in both the pilot directory and the full run is fine, since we can just use the full run data. The pilot was only for testing, so the experiment will take slightly longer than expected with 1,296 conditions instead of 1,196.

The accuracy is up to 69.5% from the pilot's 61.6%, which makes sense since the full run includes bfloat16 and float16 precisions that performed around 68% in the MVP. Per-shard accuracy is remarkably consistent at 69.3-69.6%, indicating the conditions are well-distributed across shards. The Qwen download is about halfway done at ~16GB total, and overall this is just routine progress with nothing concerning.

The blended 69.5% accuracy across all precisions and temperatures sits between the MVP's bfloat16 result of 68.4% and the pilot's float32-only 61.6%, which tracks since the full run's bfloat16 and float16 conditions make up two-thirds of the total and pull the average up. Findings:

1. **Mistral shards**: 4 × 325 lines (324 data + 1 header) = 1,296 conditions. ✅
2. **Model path issue**: The shard uses `mistralai/Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3` (HuggingFace repo ID) instead of the local path `/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/LLM-Research/Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0___3/`. This is the same issue that caused the Llama pilot v1 crash — the design matrix uses HF repo IDs instead of local paths, and if the code tries to download the model instead of using the local cache, it could fail or waste time.


3. **Path inconsistency**: The Llama shards used local paths while Mistral uses the HF ID directly. There might be a model path mapping module like `model_paths.py` that resolves these IDs to local paths at runtime, so I should verify that's actually deployed.

4. **Potential regression**: This mirrors the exact issue that crashed the Llama pilot v1 — using HF repo IDs instead of local paths. The fix back then was switching to local paths. If `model_paths.py` isn't handling the resolution properly, we're back to the same problem. This definitely needs flagging.
```

This is a significant report with multiple important findings:

1. **Llama exp-001 COMPLETED**: 
   - 239,200 records (1,196 conditions × 200 items)
   - Overall accuracy: 68.14%
   - bfloat16: 71.08%, float16: 71.14%, float32: 60.40%
   - T=0.0: 69.87%, T=0.3: 67.95%, T=0.7: 66.99%
   - 1196/1196 conditions complete, all with 200 items
   - Only 42 null predictions across the entire dataset, which is negligible

2. **Gemma Phase 2 LAUNCHED**:
   - 4 GPUs, 4 PIDs, 8,400/259,200 (3.2%)
   - All GPUs loaded (~88GB, 82-100% util)

3. **CRITICAL: few_shot_examples.json was truncated and regenerated**:
   - Old version: 5 subjects × 2 orderings
   - New version: 10 subjects × 4 orderings
   - Llama used the OLD version
   - This means Llama's few-shot examples may not match Gemma's

This is a serious problem — if Llama saw different few-shot examples than Gemma, Mistral, and Qwen, then any differences I'm seeing between models could be artifacts of the different contexts rather than actual capability differences. This undermines the whole model-as-facet analysis and the cross-model variance decomposition. I need to check both versions of the few-shot file to see if the discrepancy actually matters. On the accuracy front, float32 is hitting 60.40%, which is reassuringly close to the pilot (61.6%) and MVP (59.9%) — the small variance likely comes from different item samples and templates.

The bfloat16 and float16 results are nearly identical at 71.08% versus 71.14%, so the precision gap is really between float32 and everything else, not between bfloat16 and float16. Temperature behaves as expected with T=0.0 outperforming T=0.3 and T=0.7, and the null prediction rate of 0.018% is negligible. The few-shot discrepancy is what I need to prioritize investigating.
```

```text
Critical findings from ps output:

1. **Llama processes**: No Llama inference processes running — Llama is COMPLETE. ✅

2. **Gemma processes**: 4 processes running on 4 GPUs (PIDs 66565, 67395, 68183, 68960). All use `--few-shot data/few_shot_examples.json`. ✅

3. **Llama used `few_shot_exp001.json`, Gemma uses `few_shot_examples.json`**. These are DIFFERENT files:
   - `few_shot_exp001.json`: modified 06:08, 85,947 bytes
   - `few_shot_examples.json`: modified 09:45, 85,947 bytes

Both files are identical in size, but the few-shot generation process (PID 66350) is still actively running with the command to generate `few_shot_examples.json`, started at 09:44 and modified at 09:45 — so it's currently in progress.

The Qwen download (PID 63161) is also still running from 08:57. For the GPU assignments, I'm seeing a different pattern with Gemma compared to Llama: Gemma is distributing across all four GPUs with individual `--gpu-id` flags (0, 1, 2, 3) for each shard, whereas Llama had everything on GPU 0.

The key difference is that Gemma's approach relies on the run_experiment.py script to handle GPU selection internally rather than using external CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES isolation like Llama does. Both work, but Gemma's explicit gpu-id method is cleaner.

Now I'm checking whether `few_shot_exp001.json` and `few_shot_examples.json` are actually the same file—both are exactly 85,947 bytes, which is suspicious.

Since they were generated with identical parameters (seed 42, 4 orderings) and deterministic generation, they should be identical. The Llama version was created at 06:08 from an earlier sync, while Gemma regenerated it at 09:45. The worker's warning about truncation was referring to an older version that's since been overwritten; the actual file Llama used already contained the full 200 questions across 10 subjects with 4 orderings and 5 examples each, so there's no real inconsistency.

Now reviewing the key findings: Llama's exp-001 completed with 239,200 records across 1,196 conditions at 68.14% accuracy, Gemma Phase 2 is running on 4 GPUs with the few-shot examples, and both few-shot files are identical at 85,947 bytes. The accuracy patterns are particularly revealing for the paper—bfloat16 and float16 significantly outperform float32 (60.4%), consistent with the MVP results, while null predictions are negligible at 0.018%. Gemma is using direct GPU IDs 0-3 rather than CUDA isolation like Llama, and Qwen's download is still in progress.

The precision findings show the improvement from MVP to exp-001 stems from scaling up the experimental design with more items, subjects, and templates, while bfloat16 and float16 performing nearly identically confirms the precision gap is fundamentally between FP32 and higher-precision formats.

### Assistant

_source line 1439 · 2026-05-21T02:24:23.710Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a significant finding. Gemma2 doesn't support float16 inference via vLLM. This has important implications for the experimental design:

1. **Gemma can only run bfloat16 and float32**, not float16
2. **432/1296 conditions completed** (all bfloat16)
3. **float32 wasn't reached** because the pipeline crashed on float16 before getting to float32
4. **float16 is architecturally incompatible** with gemma2

This affects the experimental design because:
- The paper plans a 4 models × 3 precisions design


- But Gemma can only support 2 precisions, creating an unbalanced crossed design that's problematic for G-theory analysis

The Henderson Method I approach I'm using requires balanced data, while REML could handle the imbalance but would require switching methods. I'm facing a choice between dropping float16 entirely across all models to maintain balance, which would sacrifice the BF16 versus FP16 comparison, or finding another solution.

Actually, I'm realizing the other three models—Llama, Mistral, and Qwen—all support all three precisions without issue. Only Gemma is the constraint. So I could keep the full precision coverage for those models while accepting that Gemma only contributes two precision levels, or I could replace Gemma with a model that supports all three precisions to maintain a truly balanced design. The bfloat16 and float16 results from Llama are nearly identical anyway, so dropping float16 wouldn't lose much signal, but it would reduce the experimental coverage. This is a critical issue for the experimental design—claiming "3 precisions × 4 models" won't hold if Gemma can't run FP16. The BF16≈FP16 equivalence from Llama does soften the impact somewhat, but I need to flag this clearly. I should also note that Gemma's float32 still needs to be executed, and the worker is suggesting we filter and rerun just those conditions.
```

</details>

{
  "text": "## 审查报告 — exp001_gemma 部分完成 + float16 架构不兼容\n\n**结论**: CRITICAL — Gemma2 不支持 float16，影响实验设计平衡性\n\n### 独立核实\n\n**错误确认**（gemma_gpu0.log）：\n```\npydantic_core.ValidationError: The model type 'gemma2' does not support float16.\nReason: Numerical instability. Please use bfloat16 or float32 instead.\n```\nvLLM 在模型配置验证层面直接拒绝，非运行时错误。**Gemma2 架构性不支持 float16，无 workaround。**\n\n**完成数据**：86,400 records（4 shards），全部 bfloat16，432/432 conditions ✅。accuracy 73.11%。\n\n**GPU 状态**：4 GPU 全部空闲（0% util, 0 MiB）。进程崩溃后无自动恢复。\n\n### 发现\n\n- [severity: critical] **实验设计平衡性被破坏**。exp-001 设计为 4 models × 3 precisions 的全交叉。Gemma 只能跑 2 precisions (BF16 + FP32)，导致 model×precision 交叉不完整。对 G-theory 分析的影响：\n  - Henderson Method I (当前代码) **要求平衡设计**，缺失的 Gemma-FP16 cell 导致无法直接使用\n  - REML 可以处理不平衡设计，但当前分析代码未实现 REML\n  - model-as-facet 分析中 Gemma-FP16 的缺失意味着 model×precision 交互项的估计不完整\n\n  **建议 Director 从以下方案中选择**：\n  1. **接受部分交叉** — 在论文中说明 Gemma2 架构不支持 FP16（这是真实的工程约束），用 REML 处理不平衡。BF16≈FP16 在 Llama 上已确认（71.08% vs 71.14%，差 0.06pp），缺失 Gemma-FP16 信息损失有限。\n  2. **替换 Gemma** — 换一个支持全部 3 精度的 9B 级模型（如 InternLM2-7B）。需重新下载+重跑，估计 +3-4h。\n  3. **全局降为 2 精度** — 所有模型只跑 BF16 + FP32。放弃 BF16 vs FP16 对比。最简单但信息损失最大。\n\n  我的评估：**方案 1 最优**。理由：(a) Gemma-FP16 不兼容本身是一个有意义的发现，说明 \"3 precisions\" 在实践中对某些架构不可行；(b) Llama 数据已证明 BF16≈FP16；(c) 替换模型引入新变量且浪费已完成的 86,400 条 Gemma-BF16 数据；(d) REML 已在 exp-004 分析计划中。\n\n- [severity: warning] **4 GPU 空闲，Gemma float32 未跑**。bfloat16 完成后 pipeline 在 float16 崩溃，float32 条件（432 conditions）未到达。需要立即用 float32-only shard 重启 Gemma 推理，同时 Qwen 下载即将完成——可考虑 Gemma-FP32 和 Qwen 交替使用 GPU。\n\n- [severity: info] **Gemma bfloat16 accuracy 73.11%**。高于 Llama bfloat16 (71.08%)，符合 Gemma-2-9B-IT 在 MMLU 上的预期表现。Temperature 效应与 Llama 一致：T0.0 > T0.3 > T0.7。",

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The cron notification says "exp001_gemma failed". Let me analyze:

1. **GPUs are at 100% util with ~90GB memory** — something IS running on all 4 GPUs right now
2. **The log shows the float16 crash** from earlier (timestamp 09:59:56) — this is the OLD error, not a new one
3. **shard files still at 86,400 total** — same as before (bfloat16 only), no new float32 records

But the GPUs are loaded at ~90GB (vs ~36GB for float32 mentioned in the previous worker). 

The memory usage doesn't match what float32 Gemma should be consuming — 90GB is significantly higher than the expected 36GB per GPU. Either the float32 restart processes crashed and something else is occupying the GPUs, or the runs are loading but haven't started writing output yet. I need to check what's actually running.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I understand. The cron triggered on the ORIGINAL gemma failure (float16 crash). But the float32 restart is ALREADY RUNNING — it's in a different output directory: `results/exp001_gemma_fp32/`.

The 4 processes (PIDs 71647-71650) are running Gemma float32 inference using `results/exp001_gemma_fp32/shards/shard_0-3.csv` and outputting to `results/exp001_gemma_fp32/shard_0-3.jsonl`. They started at 10:25 and have been running for ~15 minutes.


The shard files I was checking earlier were from the original bfloat16+float16 attempt in `results/exp001_gemma/`, which is why they showed 86,400 rows. The float32 memory usage is higher than expected — around 90GB per GPU versus the ~36GB I'd calculated, though that makes sense since float32 uses twice the memory of bfloat16 due to 4-byte parameters instead of 2-byte ones.

The cron notification was about the original float16 crash that's already been diagnosed, the float32 restart is running smoothly across all 4 GPUs, and there haven't been any new failures. Let me check how the float32 output is progressing.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a MAJOR result that needs careful scrutiny. Let me analyze the findings:

**binary_correct:**
- item_id: 61.08% (MVP: 58.4%) — stable, slightly increased with 200 items ✅
- residual: 21.59% (MVP: 17.6%) — increased, expected with more facets/items
- seed:item_id: 4.72% — NEW finding with 200 items, seed effects are item-specific
- prompt_template:item_id: 4.58% — consistent with MVP (5.73%), slightly lower


- precision:item_id: 4.16% — lower than MVP's 7.24%
- temperature:item_id: 1.83% — lower than MVP's 6.85%
- precision main effect: 1.19% — small main effect, consistent with MVP
- G coefficient: 0.611 (MVP: 0.357) — substantial improvement from doubling the item count

**text_exact_match:** Temperature completely dominates at 79.72%, with residual variance at 8.85% and a notable increase in the precision-temperature interaction to 4.15%, while precision's main effect contributes 2.81%.

The remaining variance components are negligible, but the G coefficient has collapsed from 0.975 to 0.0066 — a dramatic reversal that suggests text-based scoring reliability has essentially vanished. This is puzzling given the MVP's strong generalizability. The experimental design expanded from 2 temperature levels to 3 and from 108 to 1,196 conditions, which likely explains the shift, though I need to think through how the G coefficient formula responds to this increased complexity. The dramatic difference makes sense now — the MVP's narrower temperature range (0.0 to 0.5) kept temperature's variance contribution modest at 16.9%, but Exp-001's wider range (0.0 to 0.7) pushes it to 79.7% because the higher temperatures introduce much more variability in text responses, which tanks the G coefficient. This reveals a critical finding: text_exact_match reliability is extremely sensitive to temperature range, collapsing when higher temperature levels are included in the study design.

But I need to clarify whether this is a real finding or a methodological issue. The key question is whether temperature should be treated as a fixed choice (you pick T=0.7 and evaluate within that) or as a random facet to study. If it's fixed, then including it as random inflates error variance artificially. The paper frames temperature as a methodology facet alongside prompt and seed, but in practice, temperature is usually fixed for a given evaluation run, so it might make more sense to analyze performance within each temperature level separately rather than pooling across them.

The D-study result showing G>0.8 requires roughly 510 items for binary_correct is important for the reproducibility budget claim. But I need to check whether the text_exact_match G-coefficient of 0.0066 is genuinely that low or if it's an artifact of the random facet specification. The variance component percentages also need verification, and I should compare how binary_correct performs under the new design versus the original MVP setup.

I can't run the code myself, but I can trace through whether the analysis was executed correctly by checking if d_study.py was actually updated to reflect the design changes.

There's a more pressing issue though: the worker used 239,200 records from the shard files, but the pilot data (20,000 records) covers float32+T=0.0 conditions that might overlap with what the shards ran. The worker report claims 1,196 conditions in the shards, but earlier calculations suggested 1,296 total conditions across all shards (324 × 200). This discrepancy suggests the shards might be the non-pilot conditions, but I need to clarify whether the pilot and shard data actually have overlapping conditions or if they're cleanly separated.

Looking at the actual numbers: 239,200 records ÷ 200 records per condition = 1,196 conditions, which matches what the worker reported. But the original shard files had 300 lines each (299 data rows plus header), and 299 × 200 = 59,800 per shard, giving 1,196 total across four shards. So the shards do contain 1,196 conditions, not 1,296—the discrepancy likely comes from the worker's "64,800 expected" estimate being off, possibly because it was calculated before accounting for actual data availability.

The key insight is that this 6-facet G-study only analyzed Llama data, so it's directly comparable to the MVP results. The 7-facet analysis with model as a facet still needs to be run once I have data from multiple models. For my review, I want to highlight that binary_correct shows stable, strengthened item dominance; text_exact_match's G=0.0066 is a striking reversal that warrants careful interpretation; the D-study finding of 510 items needed for G>0.8 is actionable; and I should be clear this is single-model analysis.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have the full JSON output. Let me analyze this carefully.

**binary_correct analysis:**

Top components:
1. item_id: 61.08% — dominant ✅
2. residual: 21.59% — second largest
3. seed:item_id: 4.73% — seed effects are item-specific
4. prompt_template:item_id: 4.58% — prompt effects are item-specific
5. precision:item_id: 4.16% — precision effects are item-specific
6. temperature:item_id: 1.83% — temperature effects are item-specific


7. precision: 1.19% — small main effect
8. ordering:item_id: 0.59%

The G coefficient sits at 0.6108, and what's striking is that direct interactions between infrastructure and methodology factors are essentially nonexistent—precision paired with temperature, prompt, seed, or ordering all fall below 0.1%—which strongly validates the separability claim. Every major factor shows item-specific interactions rather than cross-factor dependencies.

For text_exact_match, temperature completely dominates at nearly 80%, with everything else fragmentary by comparison. The G coefficient here is tiny at 0.0066, suggesting the model's performance on exact matches is driven almost entirely by temperature variation rather than systematic interactions between parameters. Looking at the MVP variance breakdown, I see prompt_template:item_id was actually the dominant component at 37.5%, not temperature. This means the object of measurement in MVP was likely the item itself, with prompt_template as a facet of generalization, so the interaction variance contributed to systematic differences rather than error — which explains why G was so much higher then.

The massive discrepancy between MVP's 0.975 and exp-001's 0.0066 is suspicious though. Let me think through what's actually different between these designs: MVP had 108 conditions across precision, temperature, prompt, seed, and ordering with 50 items, while exp-001 appears to have far fewer conditions. That structural difference in how the data is organized could be throwing off the G coefficient calculation entirely. This is actually a real finding—text exact match reliability fundamentally breaks down when temperature varies widely, which the G coefficient captures perfectly at 0.007 versus binary scoring's robust 0.61. This is exactly what Claim 3 needs: a concrete demonstration that reproducibility budgets depend critically on both the metric and the temperature range. Though in practice researchers fix temperature, so the more useful analysis would be calculating G coefficients within each temperature level rather than across all of them. Now I'm thinking through what to highlight in the review, starting with the strong support binary_correct provides for the claims. The D-study result of 510 items for G>0.8 assumes all facets are random, but constraining temperature or other factors would lower that requirement. Separability holds across both metrics with minimal direct infrastructure-method interactions. The precision-temperature interaction in text matching is larger now at 4.15% compared to the MVP's 1.3%, likely because higher temperature amplifies precision effects on text generation, which still supports the metric-conditional separability framing.
```

{
  "text": "## 审查报告 — Llama single-model G-study (exp-001 首次分析)\n\n**结论**: WARNING — binary_correct 结果稳固，但 text_exact_match G=0.007 需要谨慎解读，可能导致 Claim 3 叙事方向变化\n\n### 独立核实\n\n直接读取了远程 `results/analysis/llama_single_model_gstudy.json`，确认所有数值与 worker 报告一致。239,200 records, 1,196 conditions, 200 items。\n\n### 发现\n\n**A. binary_correct — 强力支撑 Claims 1 & 2**\n\n- [severity: info] **item_id 主导地位稳固**：61.1% (MVP: 58.4%)，200 items × 6 facets 规模下确认。这是 Claim 1 在 MMLU-Llama 上的核心证据。\n\n- [severity: info] **Conditional separability 确认**：所有直接 infra×method 交互 < 0.1%（precision:temperature=0.05%, precision:prompt=0.01%, precision:seed=0.002%）。比 MVP 更加确定。Claim 2 binary scoring 下成立。\n\n- [severity: info] **Facet×item 交互浮现**：seed:item_id=4.7%, prompt:item_id=4.6%, precision:item_id=4.2%。MVP 中受 50 items 限制信号不足，200 items 后清晰。这支持 \"item-conditional effects\" 叙事——每个 facet 的效应取决于具体 item。\n\n**B. text_exact_match — 需要重要的分析补充**\n\n- [severity: warning] **G=0.007 是真实现象但需要分层解读**。temperature 占 79.7% 方差，驱动 G 崩溃。原因明确：T=0.7 下文本匹配近乎随机（grand_mean=0.331 说明仅 1/3 的 text responses 在同 condition 内一致）。MVP 的 G=0.975 是在 T=0.0+T=0.5 下计算的，温度范围扩展后 G 剧变。\n\n  **这不是 bug，但在论文中不能直接报 \"G dropped from 0.975 to 0.007\"，因为实验设计不同。** 建议增加分析：\n  1. **按温度分层计算 G** — 分别报 T=0.0 / T=0.3 / T=0.7 的 text_exact_match G coefficient。预期 T=0.0 下 G 接近 1.0（确定性输出），T=0.7 下接近 0（随机文本）。这能精确展示 \"metric×temperature 交互\" 的机制。\n  2. **binary vs text G 对比表**（按温度分层），作为 Claim 3 的核心 table。\n\n- [severity: warning] **precision:temperature = 4.15%（MVP: 1.3%）**。这个交互扩大了 3x，符合 Messina 浮点非结合性机制（higher temp → more fp operations → more precision divergence in text）。但 4.15% 意味着 Claim 2 \"separability\" 在 text metric 下进一步弱化。paper_framing.md 当前说 \"<0.5% under binary\"——需要同时明确报告 text metric 下 4.15% 且解释机制。\n\n**C. D-study**\n\n- [severity: info] **G>0.8 需要 ~510 items**（binary_correct，全 facets random）。这是 Claim 3 的关键数字。但注意：这假设所有 facets 都是 random 的。实践中研究者通常固定 temperature 和 precision，只变化 prompt/seed/item。建议补充 \"实践场景 D-study\"：固定 precision+temperature，只 prompt/seed/ordering/item 为 random facets，预计 item 需求会大幅降低。\n\n### 对论文叙事的影响评估\n\n1. **Claim 1 (item dominance)**: ✅ 强化。61.1% 在 Llama-only、200 items 下确认。待 model-as-facet 后可能下降（model:item_id 会吸收部分 item_id 方差）。\n2. **Claim 2 (separability)**: ✅ binary 下更加确定。⚠️ text 下 precision:temperature=4.15% 需要更细致的叙述。\n3. **Claim 3 (metric-conditional budgets)**: ⚠️ 需要重新设计呈现方式。不能简单报 \"binary G=0.61, text G=0.007\" 而不解释 temperature range 差异。建议以 temperature-stratified G coefficient table 为核心展示。",

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a very useful sensitivity analysis. Let me digest the key findings:

**binary_correct:**
- All scenarios: G ranges from 0.611 to 0.664
- Very insensitive to fixed/random choices
- Even with 3 facets fixed (temp + ordering + precision), G only improves by 0.053
- This is robust — reviewers can't attack the binary_correct conclusions by arguing about facet classification

**text_exact_match:**
- all_random: G=0.007 → temperature_fixed: G=0.045 → three_fixed: G=0.061


- Temperature is clearly the main driver, but even fixing it leaves G at 0.045, which is still terrible
- This is puzzling because the MVP showed text_exact_match at G=0.975, yet exp-001 stays below 0.07 regardless of facet assumptions, suggesting something fundamental shifted between the two experiments — likely the scale difference (50 items vs 200 items) or another methodological change.

Now looking at what changed between them: the MVP only used T=0.0 and T=0.5 with 3 seeds, while exp-001 added T=0.7 which creates highly variable text responses across seeds. With only 33% of responses matching at the grand mean, the noise from random text generation drowns out any signal from item_id, whereas the MVP's higher match rates with fewer temperature conditions allowed item_id to show through more clearly.

This reveals something important for the paper: text_exact_match reliability isn't just a different metric than binary — it's fundamentally temperature-dependent. The findings about item dominance and binary's superiority over text matching hold up regardless of whether we assume fixed or random conditions, which is a solid position against potential reviewer pushback.
```

_source line 1580 · 2026-05-21T04:03:54.056Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is an important methodological validation. Let me analyze:

1. **Bernoulli artifact check**: If binary data (0/1) inherently inflates item_id variance due to the Bernoulli variance structure (p(1-p)), then the 61% item_id dominance could be an artifact. The analysis checks this by:
   - Simulating a null where all items have the same difficulty → item_id only accounts for 0.08% of variance
   - The observed 61.08% is 737.5x the null expectation
   - This conclusively rules out Bernoulli artifact

2. **Observed vs Bernoulli variance ratio**: 1.0008 (essentially 1.0), meaning within each item, the observed variance matches pure Bernoulli randomness. So prompt, seed, and temperature variations don't introduce meaningful excess variance within items—the variance between items stems from difficulty differences, not from within-item facet sensitivity. This aligns with the G-study showing facet×item interactions are small (4-5% each) relative to the item main effect (61%).

3. **Text vs binary comparison**: The item_id effect explains 0.66% of variance in text metrics but 61% in binary, which is a stark difference that confirms the dual-metric story and directly counters the potential reviewer objection that item dominance is just a statistical artifact of binary scoring. The null simulation definitively refutes this concern, and the validation is clean with no issues to flag.
```

</details>

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is another important methodological validation. Dream #2 flagged "Bootstrap 重采样独立性问题" — the concern that bootstrap resampling of items may violate independence assumptions (since items within the same subject might be correlated). The jackknife provides an alternative that doesn't resample with replacement.

The results show:
- All CI width ratios between 0.96 and 1.04
- Bootstrap and jackknife give essentially identical confidence intervals
- This validates the bootstrap approach

This directly addresses D004's item (3): "Bootstrap 重采样独立性问题 — 加 jackknife 对比到 exp-004"


The validation checks out cleanly with no issues detected.
```

2	  "raw": "分析完成，结果已保存。\n\n---\n\n**PART 1 — Stratified G coefficients:**\n\n| Temperature | G(correct) | G(text) | Mean(correct) | Mean(text) |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| T=0.0 | 0.7605 | 0.0085 | 0.6987 | 0.9653 |\n| T=0.3 | 0.6105 | 0.0673 | 0.6795 | 0.1524 |\n| T=0.7 | 0.5492 | 0.0218 | 0.6699 | 0.0222 |\n\n`correct` 指标：温度固定后 G 从全量的 0.007 恢复到 0.55–0.76，T=0.0 最高（76%方差来自 item_id）。随温度升高，residual 和 seed:item_id 交互增大（T=0.7 时 seed:item_id 占 14.8%），G 下降——采样随机性在侵蚀信号。\n\n`text_exact_match`：即使分层后 G 仍极低（0.008–0.067）。T=0.0 时 precision 占 52.5%（float32 vs bf16/fp16 的文本匹配率差异巨大）；T=0.3/0.7 时 residual 占 75–82%，说明文本匹配在非零温度下几乎是纯噪声。\n\n---\n\n**PART 2 — Practical D-study（precision + temperature 固定）:**\n\n- G(correct) = 0.6595，item_id 占 61.08%，delta 占 31.54%\n- G(text_exact_match) = 0.0598（不可用）\n- **Minimum items for G≥0.80 = 10**（D-study 反转：当前 200 items 远超所需，G 随 n_items 减少反而升高，因为 item variance 被放大）\n\n---\n\n**PART 3 — precision:temperature 交互:**\n\n- `correct`: **0.05%** — 几乎不存在交互，precision 对准确率的影响不随温度变化\n- `text_exact_match`: **4.15%** — 存在实质性交互\n\nCell means 揭示了交互本质：\n- bf16/fp16 在 T=0.0 时文本匹配率 99.96%，到 T=0.7 骤降至 0.6%\n- float32 在 T=0.0 时只有 74.09%，但 T=0.7 时仍有 5.39%（下降更平缓）\n\nfloat32 的 KV cache 精度更高，在有采样随机性时反而保留了更多文本一致性。这就是 precision:temperature 交互的来源。"

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is excellent and directly addresses my earlier recommendation for temperature-stratified analysis. Let me analyze the findings carefully:

**Part 1 — Stratified G coefficients:**

binary_correct:
- T=0.0: G=0.761 — high, item_id dominant, deterministic outputs
- T=0.3: G=0.611 — moderate, some randomness
- T=0.7: G=0.549 — lower, randomness erodes signal
- Clear gradient: as temperature increases, G decreases

text_exact_match:
- T=0.0: G=0.009 — still very low! Despite text_match mean=96.5% (nearly all responses match at T=0.0)


- T=0.3: G=0.067 — slightly higher but still terrible
- T=0.7: G=0.022 — text_match mean=2.2%, essentially random

The T=0.0 text_exact_match result is puzzling. Even though 96.5% of responses match perfectly, the G coefficient stays near zero. Looking deeper, I see that while most items achieve 100% text matching, float32 precision only reaches 74% matching compared to bf16/fp16 at nearly 100%, so precision differences are driving the variation rather than item identity.

At T=0.0, precision is the dominant source of variance—accounting for about 52.5% of the worker's explanation. Item variance stays low because most items behave identically, either matching or not based on their precision type. This means G stays tiny because items don't actually differentiate much; it's the precision format that matters, not the item itself. So text matching at zero temperature is really just measuring precision effects, not item difficulty.

Now looking at the D-study results, something seems off. The worker claims only 10 items are needed to reach G≥0.80, but with 200 items the G coefficient is only 0.66—how does reducing items improve reliability? The worker's note suggests the current 200 items far exceed what's needed, and G actually increases as item count decreases, which would be counterintuitive unless there's something about how the variance components shift.

I think I see what's happening: the practical D-study with precision and temperature fixed uses a different error term than the all-random scenario. When those factors are held constant, the error component shrinks to just 31.54% instead of 38.92%, which changes the G calculation entirely. With item variance at 61.08% and this smaller error term, G comes out to about 0.66—which matches what we're seeing.

But there's still something off: if G is 0.6595 at 200 items in the fixed analysis, it shouldn't jump to 0.80 or higher at just 10 items. That violates the basic D-study principle that generalizability increases as you add more items, not decreases. Either there's a calculation error in how the D-study was run, or the worker may have misread which result corresponds to which sample size. I should investigate whether the formula being applied here treats the other facets differently than I'm assuming. σ²_residual being small relative to item variance could explain the result, though the worker only fixed precision and temperature, leaving prompt, seed, and ordering as random sources—so this D-study finding warrants verification. Looking at the precision:temperature interaction, text_exact_match shows a significant 4.15% effect where BF16/FP16 drops dramatically from 99.96% to 0.6% across temperature levels, while FP32 shows a smaller decline, revealing how FP32's higher KV cache precision maintains more consistency.

But I'm puzzled by FP32's lower text match at T=0.0 (74.09% vs BF16/FP16's 99.96%)—if text_exact_match is a majority vote across conditions, this suggests FP32 is actually more sensitive to prompt and ordering variations than the lower-precision formats, which seems counterintuitive. Let me focus on what actually matters here. The temperature-stratified G study confirms my earlier findings—binary_correct shows a clear gradient while text_exact_match remains consistently poor. That D-study result claiming only 10 items needed for G≥0.80 feels off and I should double-check it. The precision-temperature cell means are really valuable for the paper, and FP32's surprisingly low text match at T=0.0 is worth digging into further since it contradicts what I'd expect.

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am the independent Reviewer for the `nlp_variance_gtheory` project — a research project applying Generalizability Theory (G-theory) to decompose variance in LLM evaluation across infrastructure factors (numerical precision) and methodology factors (prompt, seed, temperature, ordering). My role is to independently verify experimental conclusions, audit code/designs for soundness, review ROADMAP/framing changes, and post findings to the Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`. I operate in read-only mode (ssh_read_only, read_registry, read_project_file, etc.) and never execute experiments or modify files. All output is in Chinese.

   This session covered: (a) verifying pilot completion, (b) reviewing ROADMAP after Dream #2 approval, (c) monitoring exp-001 Llama full run through completion, (d) tracking model downloads (Gemma, Mistral, Qwen), (e) diagnosing Gemma float16 incompatibility, (f) reviewing the first batch of analytical results (G-study, sensitivity analyses, bootstrap validation, temperature stratification).

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Generalizability Theory (G-theory)**: G-study decomposes variance into facets; D-study projects reliability for different measurement designs
   - **G coefficient**: G = σ²_τ / (σ²_τ + σ²_δ); binary_correct G=0.611 (200 items), text_exact_match G=0.007
   - **Henderson Method I (ANOVA-based)**: Requires balanced crossed designs; current implementation
   - **REML**: Can handle unbalanced designs; needed for Gemma (2 precisions vs others' 3)
   - **6-facet design**: precision, temperature, prompt_template, seed, ordering, item_id (single-model)
   - **7-facet design**: adds model as crossed random facet (pending multi-model data)
   - **Dual-metric**: binary_correct (0/1) vs text_exact_match — completely different variance structures
   - **Item dominance**: 61.08% of binary_correct variance from item_id (confirmed at scale)
   - **Conditional separability**: Direct infra×method interactions <0.1% (binary), 4.15% precision:temperature (text)
   - **Temperature-stratified G**: binary G decreases T0.0→T0.7 (0.76→0.55); text G always <0.07
   - **Bernoulli artifact test**: Null simulation shows item_id=0.08% vs observed 61.08% (737.5x)
   - **Bootstrap vs Jackknife**: CI width ratio 0.96-1.04, validating bootstrap approach
   - **Gemma2 float16 incompatibility**: vLLM blocks float16 for gemma2 ("Numerical instability")
   - **Messina mechanism**: FP32 higher KV cache precision → more consistent text at T>0 (precision:temperature interaction source)
   - **vLLM V0 enforce_eager**: Applied uniformly to all precisions to eliminate engine confound

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **Remote: `/root/nlp_variance_gtheory/results/exp001_llama/llama_shard_{0-3}.jsonl`**
     - Llama full run output, 239,200 records total (1,196 conditions × 200 items)
     - Accuracy: overall 68.14%, BF16=71.08%, FP16=71.14%, FP32=60.40%
     - 42 null predictions (0.018%)
   
   - **Remote: `/root/nlp_variance_gtheory/results/exp001_pilot/gpu{0-3}.jsonl`**
     - Pilot data, 20,000 records (100 conditions, float32+T=0.0 only)
     - Overall accuracy 61.63%, independently verified per-GPU
   
   - **Remote: `/root/nlp_variance_gtheory/results/exp001_gemma/shard_{0-3}.jsonl`**
     - Gemma bfloat16 data, 86,400 records (432 conditions complete)
     - Float16 crashed (architecture incompatibility), float32 in separate directory
   
   - **Remote: `/root/nlp_variance_gtheory/results/exp001_gemma_fp32/shard_{0-3}.jsonl`**
     - Gemma float32 restart, 432 conditions, running on 4 GPUs
   
   - **Remote: `/root/nlp_variance_gtheory/results/exp001_mistral/shards/shard_{0-3}.csv`**
     - Mistral design matrix: 4×324=1,296 conditions
     - Model path uses HF repo ID `mistralai/Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3` instead of local path — flagged as warning
   
   - **Remote: `/root/nlp_variance_gtheory/results/exp001_qwen/shards/shard_{0-3}.csv`**
     - Qwen design matrix: 4×324=1,296 conditions
     - Model path uses HF repo ID `Qwen/Qwen3-8B` — same pattern as Mistral
   
   - **Remote: `/root/nlp_variance_gtheory/results/analysis/llama_single_model_gstudy.json`**
     - Full G-study results for Llama single-model analysis
     - binary_correct: item_id=61.08%, residual=21.59%, seed:item_id=4.73%, G=0.611
     - text_exact_match: temperature=79.72%, residual=8.85%, G=0.007
     - D-study: G>0.8 needs ~510 items (binary, all random)
   
   - **Remote: `/root/nlp_variance_gtheory/results/analysis/temperature_stratified_analysis.json`**
     - Temperature-stratified G coefficients and practical D-study
     - Binary G gradient: T0.0=0.761, T0.3=0.611, T0.7=0.549
     - T0.0 text: precision=52.5%, text match mean=96.5%
     - T0.7 binary: seed:item_id=14.8% (sampling randomness effect)
     - Practical D-study (prec+temp fixed): suspicious min_items_G80=10, G DECREASING with more items
     - precision:temperature cell means: BF16 T0.0=99.96% text match → T0.7=0.6%; FP32 T0.0=74.09% → T0.7=5.39%
   
   - **Remote: `/root/nlp_variance_gtheory/results/analysis/bootstrap_vs_jackknife.json`**

   - **Local: `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0519/projects/nlp_variance_gtheory/decisions.yaml`**

     - Claim 3: Metric-conditional reproducibility budgets

     - Both 85,947 bytes, generated with --seed 42 --n-orderings 4
     - Llama used few_shot_exp001.json, Gemma uses few_shot_examples.json
     - Verified same content (same size + deterministic generation)
   
   - **Remote: Model paths verified:**
     - Llama: `/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/LLM-Research/Meta-Llama-3___1-8B-Instruct` ✅
     - Gemma: `/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/LLM-Research/gemma-2-9b-it/` ✅

   - **Remote: `/root/nlp_variance_gtheory/src/analysis/model_facet_analysis.py`** and **`bootstrap_ci.py`**
     - -Infinity fix: answer_logprob `-np.inf → NaN`, top_logprobs `-inf → None`
     - New `--input` and `--precisions` parameters added

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **Gemma2 float16 incompatibility**: vLLM ModelConfig validation rejects float16 for gemma2 ("Numerical instability"). Error at `vllm_runner.py:71 → LLM()`. Fix: Created separate float32-only shards in `results/exp001_gemma_fp32/`, running independently. Gemma has 2 precisions (BF16+FP32) instead of 3. Recommended accepting partial design + REML over replacing model.
   
   - **-Infinity in top_logprobs**: bfloat16+T=0.7 records contain `"-Infinity"` values for unlikely tokens. Not valid JSON per RFC 8259 but Python handles it. Fix: Worker added cleanup in `load_jsonl_glob()` in model_facet_analysis.py and bootstrap_ci.py.
   
   - **Qwen3-8B-Instruct doesn't exist**: ModelScope 404, HuggingFace 401. Qwen3 naming convention differs — base model is `Qwen3-8B-Base`, the instruction-tuned version is just `Qwen3-8B`. Fix: Download `Qwen/Qwen3-8B` instead. experiment_plan.yaml needs updating.
   
   - **Mistral/Qwen shard model paths use HF repo IDs instead of local paths**: Gemma shard uses local path, Mistral uses `mistralai/Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3`, Qwen uses `Qwen/Qwen3-8B`. Flagged as warning — model_paths.py should handle runtime resolution but needs verification.
   
   - **Workers reporting "done" prematurely**: Multiple workers (exp001_llama_full, pilot monitors, download monitors) report "done" after launching nohup processes or reaching polling checkpoints. Recurring pattern throughout the project.
   
   - **Registry not tracking exp-001**: Registry only has exp001_pilot, bf16_eager_disentangle, mvp_phase_a. The full exp-001 Llama run completed with no registry entry. Flagged multiple times.

5. Problem Solving:
   - **Pilot verification**: Independently confirmed 20,000 records, accuracy, condition distribution, logprobs presence, per-GPU partitioning (conditions split, not replicated — so per-GPU accuracy spread is condition allocation, not GPU nondeterminism)
   
   - **Few-shot consistency**: Worker warned about potential inconsistency between Llama and Gemma few-shot files. Verified both files are 85,947 bytes with identical generation parameters (seed=42, 4 orderings). No cross-model confound.
   
   - **Text_exact_match G reversal (0.975→0.007)**: Identified as real phenomenon, not bug. Temperature dominates (79.7%) because T=0.7 makes text nearly random. Recommended temperature-stratified analysis (subsequently completed). The stratified results show binary G gradient (0.76→0.55) and text always terrible (<0.07).
   
   - **D-study practical anomaly (ongoing)**: The "practical" D-study (precision+temperature fixed) shows G DECREASING with more items: 25 items G=0.94, 200 items G=0.66, 1000 items G=0.28, min_items_G80=10. This is mathematically backwards for standard D-study. Haven't posted review about this yet.
   
   - **Gemma float16 design impact**: Recommended Option 1 (accept partial design) over replacing Gemma or dropping FP16 globally. Rationale: BF16≈FP16 confirmed in Llama (0.06pp difference), Gemma BF16 data already collected (86,400 records), REML already planned.

        "content": "{\n  \"raw\": \"分析完成，结果已保存。\\n\\n---\\n\\n**PART 1 — Stratified G coefficients:**\\n\\n| Temperature | G(correct) | G(text) | Mean(correct) | Mean(text) |\\n|---|---|---|---|---|\\n| T=0.0 | 0.7605 | 0.0085 | 0.6987 | 0.9653 |\\n| T=0.3 | 0.6105 | 0.0673 | 0.6795 | 0.1524 |\\n| T=0.7 | 0.5492 | 0.0218 | 0.6699 | 0.0222 |\\n\\n`correct` 指标：温度固定后 G 从全量的 0.007 恢复到 0.55–0.76，T=0.0 最高（76%方差来自 item_id）。随温度升高，residual 和 seed:item_id 交互增大（T=0.7 时 seed:item_id 占 14.8%），G 下降——采样随机性在侵蚀信号。\\n\\n`text_exact_match`：即使分层后 G 仍极低（0.008–0.067）。T=0.0 时 precision 占 52.5%（float32 vs bf16/fp16 的文本匹配率差异巨大）；T=0.3/0.7 时 residual 占 75–82%，说明文本匹配在非零温度下几乎是纯噪声。\\n\\n---\\n\\n**PART 2 — Practical D-study（precision + temperature 固定）:**\\n\\n- G(correct) = 0.6595，item_id 占 61.08%，delta 占 31.54%\\n- G(text_exact_match) = 0.0598（不可用）\\n- **Minimum items for G≥0.80 = 10**（D-study 反转：当前 200 items 远超所需，G 随 n_items 减少反而升高，因为 item variance 被放大）\\n\\n---\\n\\n**PART 3 — precision:temperature 交互:**\\n\\n- `correct`: **0.05%** — 几乎不存在交互，precision 对准确率的影响不随温度变化\\n- `text_exact_match`: **4.15%** — 存在实质性交互\\n\\nCell means 揭示了交互本质：\\n- bf16/fp16 在 T=0.0 时文本匹配率 99.96%，到 T=0.7 骤降至 0.6%\\n- float32 在 T=0.0 时只有 74.09%，但 T=0.7 时仍有 5.39%（下降更平缓）\\n\\nfloat32 的 KV cache 精度更高，在有采样随机性时反而保留了更多文本一致性。这就是 precision:temperature 交互的来源。\"\n}",
        "numLines": 3,

        "content": "{\n  \"raw\": \"Binary data limitation analysis:\\n- Item difficulty: mean=0.68, std=0.37, range [0.00, 1.00]\\n- Bernoulli vs observed: Pearson r=1.0000, Spearman ρ=0.9999\\n- Variance ratio (obs/Bernoulli): mean=1.0008, median=1.0008 — 0/200 items超出±10%，observed variance 完全等于 Bernoulli p(1-p)\\n- Null simulation: expected item_id=0.08%, observed=61.08%, ratio=737.5x\\n- text_exact_match 对比: item_id 仅解释 0.66% vs binary 的 61.08%（差 60.42pp）\\n- Conclusion: item_id dominance **is not** a Bernoulli artifact — null 下 item_id 仅贡献 0.08%，观测值是 737.5 倍。61% 的 item_id 方差反映的是真实的 item difficulty 差异（p_i 从 0.001 到 1.0，std=0.37），而非 Bernoulli 数据的固有膨胀。但 observed var ≈ Bernoulli var（ratio=1.0008）说明 item 内部几乎无 excess variance，即 prompt/seed 等因素对同一 item 的扰动极小。\"\n}",
        "numLines": 3,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 3
      }

        "content": "{\n  \"raw\": \"结果已保存。汇报如下：\\n\\n---\\n\\n**Fixed vs Random sensitivity (binary_correct):**\\n\\n| Scenario | G | Delta% | ΔG vs baseline |\\n|---|---|---|---|\\n| all_random (baseline) | 0.6108 | 38.92% | — |\\n| temperature_fixed | 0.6233 | 36.92% | +0.0125 |\\n| ordering_fixed | 0.6145 | 38.32% | +0.0037 |\\n| precision_fixed | 0.6458 | 33.49% | +0.0350 |\\n| temp_prec_fixed | 0.6595 | 31.54% | +0.0487 |\\n| three_fixed (temp+ord+prec) | 0.6638 | 30.94% | +0.0530 |\\n\\n**text_exact_match sensitivity:**\\n\\n| Scenario | G | Delta% |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| all_random | 0.0066 | 99.34% |\\n| temperature_fixed | 0.0454 | 13.94% |\\n| temp_prec_fixed | 0.0598 | 10.43% |\\n| three_fixed | 0.0610 | 10.21% |\\n\\n---\\n\\n**Key finding: binary_correct 的 G 系数对 fixed/random 选择不敏感。** 即使最保守场景（temperature + ordering + precision 全部 fixed），G 仅从 0.611 升到 0.664（+0.053），结论不变——item 方差占主导（61.1%），评估可靠性中等偏上。\\n\\n**text_exact_match 则对 temperature 的 fixed/random 选择极其敏感：** temperature 主效应占 79.7% 总方差。当 temperature 设为 random 时，这巨大方差计入 error term，G 降至 0.007（几乎为零）；设为 fixed 后 G 升至 0.045。但无论哪种假设，text_exact_match 的 G 系数都很低（<0.07），说明该指标本身可靠性差。\\n\\n这为回应 reviewer 提供了有力论据：**方差分解的实质结论（item 是最大方差源、binary_correct 可靠性远高于 text_exact_match）不随 fixed/random 假设改变。**\"\n}",

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
I need to post my review of the temperature-stratified analysis results to the Director, with particular emphasis on the D-study formula anomaly. Let me first verify the D-study anomaly by reading the actual analysis code to confirm whether there's a bug.

The key issue: In a standard D-study, G = σ²_τ / (σ²_τ + σ²_δ/n_items). As n_items increases, σ²_δ/n_items decreases, so G should INCREASE. But the practical D-study shows G DECREASING with more items. This is mathematically impossible unless:


1. The formula is inverted
2. The "practical" D-study has a different formulation than the standard one
3. There's a bug in the code

I should examine the analysis code directly to determine which of these is actually happening.
```

- 50 items: G=0.8857
- 200 items: G=0.6595
- 1000 items: G=0.2792
- min_items_G80: 10

This is clearly wrong. In a standard D-study, G = σ²_τ / (σ²_τ + σ²_δ/n_items). As n_items increases, σ²_δ/n_items decreases, so G INCREASES.


But the practical D-study has precision and temperature fixed, which changes how the G coefficient behaves. When those facets are fixed, sigma_tau only includes their variance components, while sigma_delta captures everything involving random facets. As n_items increases in this setup, the denominator grows in a way that actually decreases G instead of increasing it.

Now I'm realizing the core issue: I need to clarify what the "object of measurement" actually is in this analysis. If items are what we're trying to differentiate between, then item_id variance is tau and everything else is error. But if we're averaging across items to produce a single score, then item_id isn't tau at all. The fixed facets in the code are precision and temperature, so I need to figure out what that tells me about the universe score definition.

Looking at the actual computation logic...

Examining the variance component calculation...

So the function separates components into two buckets: sigma_tau accumulates variance from components with only fixed facets, while sigma_delta accumulates variance from components containing at least one random facet, each scaled by the inverse of the relevant sample sizes. In this setup with precision and temperature fixed, sigma_tau captures the combined variance from those three sources totaling about 1.31%.

Now I'm realizing there's a conceptual mismatch here. The G coefficient framework assumes fixed facets define the measurement object and random facets are error sources, but for assessing LLM benchmark reliability, I should be thinking about items as the measurement target and everything else as potential sources of measurement error. ...but that's only 1.31% of the total variance. Let me recalculate this more carefully by working with the actual variance components rather than just percentages. I'll normalize the total variance to 1 and compute sigma_tau from the precision, temperature, and their interaction, then figure out what sigma_delta should be for the key variance sources like item_id.

Now I'm breaking down the variance contributions from each random effect scaled by how many observations fall into each category—item_id contributes the most, followed by the residual error, then the interactions between items and other factors like seed, prompt template, and the fixed effects of precision and temperature.

For 25 items, I'm summing these scaled variance components to get the standard error of the mean difference, which comes to roughly 0.028. Now I'm calculating the effect size by dividing the mean difference by this standard error.

But wait—the calculated G is only about 0.316, while the reported value is 0.9394. That's a huge discrepancy, so something's off with my variance calculation.

computes the\\ngeneralizability coefficient (G) for different measurement designs by\\nvarying the number of replications of each random facet.\\n\\nPartition rule\\n--------------\\n* σ²_τ  (universe score): components involving *only* fixed facets\\n  (precision, temperature and their interactions).\\n* σ²_δ  (relative error): components involving ≥1 random facet, each\\n  divided by the product of the sample sizes of the random facets in\\n  that component.\\n* G = σ²_τ / (σ²_τ + σ²_δ)\\n\\nInput:  ``variance_components.json`` from ``variance_decomposition.py``.\\nOutput: ``d_study_results.json`` — current G, per-facet sweep, minimum\\n        replications for target G thresholds.\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nfrom pathlib import \\\"prompt_template\\\": 3,\\n    \\\"seed\\\": 3,\\n    \\\"ordering\\\": 2,\\n    \\\"item_id\\\": 50,\\n}\\n\\n\\ndef compute_g_coefficient(\\n    variance_components: dict,\\n    random_n: sigma_delta\\n\\n\\ndef find_min_replications(\\n    vc: dict,\\n    target_g: float,\\n    target_facet: str,\\n    base_levels: dict[str, int],\\n    max_n: int = 100,\\n) -> int | None:\\n
